NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Fern Benally • Alberto L. Peshlakai •Jason E. Whiting • Daryl Seymore • Dawnafe Whitesinger

NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
Tuesday, January 23, 2024
NAVAJO COUNTY GOVERNMENTAL COMPLEX
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS' CHAMBERS
100 EAST CODE TALKERS DRIVE
HOLBROOK, AZ 86025
One or more supervisors may attend telephonically
Pursuant to A.R.S. §38-431.02(H), the public body will have physical access to the meeting place one (1) hour prior
to the start of the meeting. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive session to discuss certain matters and
for legal advice on any item as indicated on the following agenda, pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A)(3). Items on
the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairperson's discretion. A copy of the agenda background
material provided to the Board, except items to be considered in the executive session, is available for public
inspection at the Navajo County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
WATCH THE MEETING LIVE AT: www.navajocountyaz.gov/660
All public comments will need to be made in person or in writing. Written comments will be received by the
Clerk of the Board's office twenty-four (24) hours prior to the Board meeting, at 
melissa.buckley@navajocountyaz.gov. If further accommodation is needed, please contact the Clerk of the
Board's office, at (928) 524-4053 twenty-four (24) hours prior to the Board Meeting, so accommodation can
be arranged.  
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of
the Board’s office at least forty-eight (48) hours prior to the meeting, so accommodation can be arranged.
“NOTICE TO PARENTS AND LEGAL GUARDIANS: Parents and legal guardians have the right to consent before Navajo
County makes a video or voice recording of a minor child, pursuant to A.R.S. § 1-602(A)(9). The Navajo County Board of
Supervisors' regular meetings are recorded and may be viewed on Navajo County’s website and social media pages. If you permit
your child to attend/participate in a televised Navajo County Board of Supervisors meeting, a recording will be made, and your
child’s picture may be posted on Navajo County’s social media pages. You may exercise your right not to consent by not
allowing your child to attend/participate in the meeting.”

NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES
Tuesday, January 9, 2024
Present: Fern Benally, Vice-Chairwoman; Alberto Peshlakai, Supervisor, District
II; Jason Whiting, Chairman; Daryl Seymore, Supervisor District IV;
Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor District V 
Staff

9:00 a.m
. Call To Order: Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance was led by Supervisor
Alberto Peshlakai.
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:
a. Election of Chairman and Vice-Chairman for 2024:
Supervisor Seymore made a motion to recommend Supervisor
Whiting as Chairman for 2024, motion seconded by Supervisor
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.
Supervisor Whiting made a motion to recommend Supervisor Benally
as Vice-Chairman for 2024, motion seconded by Supervisor Seymore.
Vote unanimous approving the motion.
b. Presentation to Chairman Alberto Peshlakai for his 2023 service:
Chairman Whiting thanked the public for their trust and indicated that
it’s always a pleasure to work with the Board.
Chairman Whiting presented a plaque to the outgoing chair
Supervisor Alberto Peshlakai and recognized him for his great
leadership. He stated that his leadership was valued and appreciated
and that he did a great job as Chairman.
Supervisor Whitesinger spoke of Supervisor Peshlakai and his work
in the tribal communities during his employment even prior to him
becoming a supervisor, stating that he was committed to making sure
the organization was well represented. She indicated that she
appreciates the way that he serves his community and the county as a
whole. She thanked him for his service.
Supervisor Seymore shared his appreciation to Supervisor Peshlakai
and thanked him for his service and diligence during 2024.
Vice-Chairwoman Benally spoke of Supervisor Peshlakai and their
work together on the Board. She thanked him for his leadership.
Chairman Whiting indicated that Supervisor Peshlakai not only
represents his district but Navajo County as a whole and he is grateful to
him for that.
CALL TO THE PUBLIC:
There were no public comments.

BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:
 
a. Presentation and update from Heber-Overgaard Community Resource
Network:
A presentation was shown.  John Piva, a Board of Director for HOCRN,
stated that the Heber-Overgaard Community Resource Network is a
non-profit 501c3 serving the Heber-Overgaard residents and visitors, that
it is fully operated by volunteers, that they also operate a thrift store and a
community van.  He stated that they were established in 2022 by former
Fire Chief McCluskey and local Realtor, Jane Call, that they were
originally established to handle emergency and non-emergency requests
from the fire and sheriff departments and that their Mission Statement is
“To provide coordination of community resources for Heber-Overgaard
residents and visitors”.  He talked about taking over an existing Thrift
store, indicating that it is run fully by volunteers.  He further talked about
their community garden project, indicating that they had a local gardening
expert as well as two master gardeners who offered guidance when they
  
                                                                    2 of 6 
DRAFT

<!-- page 9 -->
first started, that they have a twenty-year facility use agreement with the
Heber-Overgaard schools for the location, that the space allows for
growth and that the volunteers built the garden from the ground up.  He
addressed the funds that have been raised from the garden and where
the funds came from.  He talked about what all the garden consists of,
being able to become a Friend of the Garden (FOG) and the benefits that
membership provides and some of the classes that are offered at the
garden.  He introduced the members of the HOCRN Board of Directors
and talked about the services they provide to the community.  He talked
about the future plans for HOCRN.  He talked about their logo, indicating
that the wagon wheel represents all of their resources.  He reviewed the
statistics for HOCRN for 2023.  He talked about the different ways that the
organization can be contacted. 
Supervisor Seymore talked about the integrity of the HOCRN
organization, indicating that they do what they say they’ll do.  He stated
that they are one of the greatest organizations that has been put together
by people who care about people. He indicated that Heber-Overgaard is
the gate to Navajo County.  He talked about the many accomplishments
in the community such as the High School Football Team State
Championships, the fantastic basketball program, and the community
programs for the Senior Center, Fire Department and Chamber of
Commerce.  He talked about their 4 th of July and Holiday festivities,
indicating that there are no city funds, it is just the citizens who love their
community and are doing things on their own to give back to their
community. He talked about the need for a program to help the
communities, such as Heber-Overgaard, that are doing things to improve
on their own to improve the communities who don’t receive city/town
funds.  He talked about the different requests where help can be provided
to give back and help make the community better.  Supervisor Seymore
thanked Mr. Piva and the HOCRN Board for what they do.
Supervisor Whitesinger thanked the HOCRN Board members for being
present today and for sharing the work that they do. She spoke of the
work that they do and commended each of them for their care and
dedication to their community and being willing to give back in so many
ways.
Chairman Whiting thanked the HOCRN Board for their service and
indicated that this is what we should aspire to do and be in our
communities.

a. Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 01-2024, granting a
Right-of-Way Abandonment of an unused stub road between lots 214 &
215 and North from the northern edge of right-of way for Wild Game Trail:
William Flake discussed the item indicating that Public Works received an
application for an abandonment of a stub road due to there not being a
need for an access point through the subdivision.  He stated that this has
been advertised and Public Works did not receive any information back
either for or against this item. He stated that staff is recommending
approval.

property owners are in agreement to purchase the right of way so there

1 
Agreement 
 
Between 
Northeast Arizona Family Resource Center, dba Navajo County Family Advocacy 
Center (the FAC) 
And 
Navajo County, Arizona 
Dated: January 1, 2024 
 
Background 
     The FAC, located in Show Low, Arizona is a non-profit organization providing a 
variety of services to child victims of sexual and physical abuse.  Services are also 
offered to victims’ families.  The FAC also provides sexual abuse education to various 
parties and law enforcement training regarding sexual and physical abuse.   
     The FAC’s income consists primarily of (1) individual, commercial and non-profit 
organizations’ contributions, (2) community fundraising events, and (3) grants from 
various corporate, charitable, and governmental sources. 
     The FAC’s fulltime staff, the Director of Services and Development Coordinator, and 
their compensation as of January 1, 2024 are employees of Navajo County and receive 
Navajo County employee benefits. 
     The FAC and Navajo County wish to enter into an agreement whereby the FAC will 
reimburse Navajo County for a portion of the compensation.   
Agreement 
     In return for Navajo County employing the individuals, the FAC agrees to pay to 
Navajo County an amount equal to 50 percent of the salary and ERE compensation. 
     The FAC will pay to Navajo County every calendar quarter in arrears, the FAC’s 
share paid by Navajo County during the quarter.  Such payment will be made within 
30 days of the end of each calendar quarter. 
    The FAC and Navajo County may change at any time the reimbursement percentage 
stated above.  Both parties also recognize that the FAC may employ individuals who 
are not subject to this Agreement.   
     Either party may, upon 30 days written notice, terminate this Agreement. 
     The parties may amend this Agreement at any time.

<!-- page 16 -->
2 
     This Agreement is effective January 1, 2024.

John Larsen Letter of Interest

January 15, 2024 
 
Cody Cooper 
Planning Manager 
Navajo County – Planning & Development Services Division 
P.O. Box 668 
Holbrook, AZ 86025 
 
Re:  Interest in Service as a Board Member on Board of Adjustment (BOA) 
 
Dear Cody, 
 
As you are aware, I spoke with Daryl Seymore a couple of weeks ago about the possibility of 
serving on the Navajo County B oard of Adjustment (BOA).  I am grateful for the opportunity to 
submit this Letter of Interest for your consideration. It would be an honor for me to be considered 
for the opportunity to serve as a member of the Navajo County BOA.  
 
I have lived in Navajo County since 1995.  My wife and I have raised four children here, three of 
whom have graduated local schools and gone off to University.  I have one child left at home who 
will be graduating high school at the end of May.  I have 30+ years of experience in construction, 
particularly in framing, insulation, and sales of all construction materials.  I also have about the 
same amount of years of experience managing people and companies, including budgets.  I have 
over twelve years of experience working on a board, which I think will be invaluable, if chosen.  I 
would also like you to know that I hold  a Bachelor of Science Degree in Applied Management 
from Grand Canyon University.  These and my other life experiences should serve me well on this 
Board.  I will be able to draw on many correlations, parallels and relevant matters from past 
projects that I have worked on throughout my professional career that will enable me to make 
sound decisions on the cases heard by this Board.  I will also look forward to the opportunities for 
learning and growth that this experience will surely provide.  I have a deep love for this area, and 
I will bring a sincere interest in serving Navajo County and its citizens in this capacity.   Integrity 
is of utmost importance to me, so I will bring an unbiased and open mind to all ca ses presented 
before the Board.  With my background and the professional knowledge I have gained throughout 
my career, I believe I am a good fit for the Navajo County BOA.  
 
In closing, I would like to thank you again for your consideration to be a member of this Board. It 
would be an hono r to represent and serve Navajo County in this capacity.   I welcome the 
opportunity, and should I be selected, I will carry out the duties and responsibilities with integrity 
and to the greatest of my abilities. Thank you for your consideration and I look forward to talking 
with you soon. 
 
Sincerely, 
 
John Larsen

Meeting Date: 01/23/2024  
Title: Amendment No. 1 to Wilson & Company Professional Services Contract District II
Submitted For: William Flake, Assistant County Engineer 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Amendment Number 1 to Wilson & Company Professional Services Contract for the Joseph
City Pedestrian Improvements Project
Background:
On May 23, 2023 the Board of Supervisors approved a Professional Services Contract with
Wilson & Company to prepare a set of construction documents for the Joseph City Pedestrian
Improvement Project.  This project will provide recommended improvements to enhance the
pedestrian circulation between the two schools, Joseph City Elementary School and Joseph City
JR/SR High School, and improve pedestrian crossings at specified locations.  It has been
determined the best sidewalk design to allow access to the most students would be to add
additional sidewalk to the project.  Amendment No. 1 to this contract adds the additional
sidewalk to the scope of work and increases the contract amount an additional $34,843.16.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 2023-24
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 34843.16
Fiscal Impact:
Funds are budgeted under Professional Services.

SUBJECT:  Scope and Fee Proposal – CONTRACT MODIFICATION 1 
Joseph City Pedestrian Improvements project 
(School Route, Sidewalk & Crosswalk Improvements) 
 
Dear Mr. Mundle: 
 
Wilson & Company, Inc., Engineers & Architects (Wilson & Company) is pleased to submit this 
Contract Modification proposal to provide the additional scope, schedule, and cost estimate to 
provide professional engineering services and consulting services to prepare a final design and 
construction documents, cost estimate, complete the environmental compliance, and utility and 
right-of-way clearances for the Joseph City School Route, Sidewalk & Crosswalk Improvements 
project funded by a Community Development Block Grant and additionally, the Arizona 
Transportation Alternatives Grant.  
 
Thank you very much for this opportunity to expand the project limits and continue working with 
you on this project. We are confident you will be pleased with the quality, timeliness, and 
responsiveness of our services and work documents. 
 
PROJECT LOCATION AND LIMITS 
 
Joseph City is an unincorporated community located in Navajo County, Arizona. It is located along 
Interstate 40 approximately eighty miles east of Flagstaff and ten miles west of Holbrook. Joseph 
City recorded a population of 1,908 inhabitants in 2020 according to the U.S. Census Bureau. 
 
The proposed expanded project limits are within the original project are shown below within the 
orange boundary depicted below in Figure 1. The project will provide recommended improvements 
to enhance the pedestrian circulation between the two schools, Joseph City Elementary School, and 
Joseph City JR/SR High School) and improve pedestrian crossings at specified locations. During the 
development of the initially understood segments of roadway that were to be improved with 
concrete sidewalk. We were informed that some additional segments to be added to the project 
would be a benefit to the community. This proposal of the contract modification outlines the five 
(5) additional segments. We understand that these additional segments are described as followed: 
1.) Construct sidewalk along the south side of 4th North Ave from Westover Avenue to Randall 
Ave. (approx. 2,250 linear feet (LF)) 
2.) Construct sidewalk along the east side of Bushman Ave from Main Street to 2nd North Ave 
(approx. 950 LF) 
3.) Construct sidewalk along the north side of 1st North Ave from Bushman Ave to Westover 
Ave (approx. 500 LF) (Note this extends the previously scoped improvement on 1st North 
Ave to the west) 
4.) Construct sidewalk along the south side of 3rd North Ave from Randall Ave to the north end 
High School Driveway (approx. 240 LF)

<!-- page 22 -->
Navajo County Public Works  
Joseph City Pedestrian Improvements Project 
pg. 2 
 
5.) Construct sidewalk along the west side of Randall Ave from 3rd North Ave to 4th North Ave 
(approx. 500 LF) (This segment would close the gap between the added improvements along 
4th North Ave to the originally proposed improvements along Randall Ave and 3rd North 
Ave. 
Improvements will include sidewalk connections to the existing road will be at grade. Raised curb 
and gutter, curb ramps at intersections are not anticipated, but may be considered if warranted. 
 
 
Figure 1 - Project Area 
 
SCOPE OF WORK 
Wilson & Company will perform the professional services required to prepare a set of construction 
documents which consist of sidewalk plans for an additional 4,500 linear feet of new sidewalk. 
Wilson & Company will combine the additional work to the already developed design under the 
original contract which included approximately 7,100 linear feet of new concrete sidewalk. The final 
plan set will include construction detail sheets at intersection and driveway crossings, in addition to, 
signing and pavement marking plans. While performing these services, Wilson & Company will use 
engineering judgment, practices and principles that could be reasonably expected from other 
engineers under like circumstances. 
 
Based on the information above the following scope of services, project schedule and fee proposal 
are as followed: 
 
PROJECT SCOPE OF WORK 
 
A. PROJECT MANAGEMENT/MEETINGS/COORDINATION 
a. Project set-up – Manage contract agreement, process invoicing, and prepare 
progress reports.  
b. Meetings/communication – All meetings are assumed to be virtual using 
Microsoft Teams or similar software application unless otherwise noted (on-site). 
The following additional meetings are anticipated as part of this contract 
modification: 
i. Resubmittal of 90% Plans, Specifications, & Estimate review meeting – 2 
staff x 1 meeting.

<!-- page 23 -->
Navajo County Public Works  
Joseph City Pedestrian Improvements Project 
pg. 3 
 
 
B. DATA COLLECTION & FIELD INVESTIGATION 
 
After a notice-to-proceed has been issued for this CONTRACT MODIFICATION #1, Wilson 
& Company will request the following items from the COUNTY to provide: 
• Any additional background information that covers the new limits that were not 
acquired for the original scoped project. This is including any relevant GIS files, but 
not limited to parcels, utilities, etc. 
 
Topographical Survey:  Wilson & Company will perform topographical survey based on the 
additional limits described up above and that were confirmed with having discussions with the 
COUNTY. The survey limits will include capturing existing features from the edge of the 
roadway to the existing right-of-way limits on the side of the roadway where the proposed 
sidewalk improvements are proposed to be constructed. Additional field shots required at street 
intersections at the proposed pedestrian crossing are recommended to provide street crossing 
designs. The additional estimated fee for the proposed topographical survey services is included 
in this proposal.  
 
Utilities:  Wilson & Company will request from AZ 811 the list of utility owners and their 
facilities within the additional project limits. Wilson & Company will be responsible for 
obtaining no-conflict clearance letters from the utility owners based on the 90% design concept. 
The COUNTY will assist in coordination efforts, on an as-needed basis. 
 
C. SCOPING DESIGN 
 
No additional scoping or conceptual design efforts are anticipated under this Contract 
Modification #1 scope of work. 
 
D. FINAL DESIGN 
 
Wilson & Company will develop the new segments of sidewalk improvements to the 90% like 
the original segments that were developed under the original scope of work. These new 
segments will be incorporated into a new complete plan set that combines all the improvements.  
 
a. 90% Design Plans, Specifications & Estimate –Wilson & Company will prepare 
90% design plans, specifications, and estimate. It is assumed that standard 
construction specifications will be used, and only special provisions will be required 
to be developed by Wilson & Company, if needed. The design plans will include the 
recommended locations for pedestrian improvements, including sidewalk, 
intersection crossings and sign and pavement markings. If warranted, Wilson & 
Company will utilize available aerial imagery for background and supplemented 
topographical survey to show proposed improvements. A detailed construction cost 
estimate will be developed at this stage. 
 
Construction plans for REVISED 90%, and 100% anticipate including the following 
sheets: 
▪ Cover Sheet (1 sheet)- REVISE & UPDATE 
▪ General Notes (1 sheet)- REVISE & UPDATE

<!-- page 24 -->
Navajo County Public Works  
Joseph City Pedestrian Improvements Project 
pg. 4 
 
▪ Keymap/Survey Control (1 sheet) REVISE & UPDATE 
▪ Typical Sections (1 sheets) – Add additional sheet to include Bushman & 4th 
North Avenues (2 total sheets) 
▪ Sidewalk plans will be 1” = 20’ scaled plans showing 1,500 linear feet per sheet 
(3-stacked plan over plan) No profiles are included. (7 sheets total) 
o 1st North Ave: Westover Ave to Randall Ave (2,250 LF = 2 sheets) Add 
the additional 500 LF to these sheets (1st North Ave, from 
Bushman Ave to Westover Ave) 
o 3rd North Ave: Westover Ave to Randal Ave (2.250 LF = 2 sheets) Add 
the extension of 500 LF to north driveway to Joseph City High School 
o Westover Ave; B-40 (Main St) to 2nd North Ave (1,000 LF = 1 sheet) 
o Shelby Ave: B-40 (Main St) to 2nd North Ave (1,000LF = 1 sheet) 
o Randall Ave: 1st North Ave to 3rd North Ave (1,000 LF = 1 sheet) Add 
the additional 500 LF to this sheet (3rd North Ave to 4th North Ave) 
o 4th North Ave, Westover Ave to Randall Ave (2,250 LF = 2 sheets) 
o Bushman Ave, B-40 (Main St) to 2nd North Ave (1,000LF = 1 sheet) 
▪ Miscellaneous Intersection & Driveway Details (5 sheets) REVISE & 
UPDATE (3 new sheets) (Estimated 8 sheets total) 
▪ Typical Traffic Control Work Zone Details (Phasing not included) (2 sheets) 
REVISE & UPDATE 
▪ Signing and Pavement Marking plans (3 Sheets) REVISE & UPDATE (2 new 
sheets) (Estimated 5 sheets total) 
 
Project Specifications will be prepared for items not covered by local construction 
standards and details acceptable to Navajo County Public Works.  
 
Cost Estimate – Project quantities will be prepared for the project and put into a bid 
schedule. Unit prices will be developed based upon available historical bid tabulations.  
 
Deliverable: REVISED 90% Design Plans, Specifications, and Estimate (11”x17” (20 scale) PDF) 
Comment Resolution meeting with the County to address design. (Virtual meeting) 
 
b. 100% Final Design Plans, Specifications & Estimate – Wilson & Company will 
address the REVISED 90% comments and the original 90% comments from the 
County and provide final plans based on the agreed resolution. 
 
There is no additional fee required in this Contract Modification #1 beyond 
what was already included in the original proposal. The deliverables remain the 
same as previously stated in the original scope and listed below. 
 
Deliverable: 100% Sealed Plans, Specifications, and Estimate (11”x17” (20 scale) PDF) 
Comment Resolution meeting with the County to address design. (Virtual meeting) 
 
E. QUALITY ASSURANCE / QUALITY CONTROL (QA/QC) 
Wilson & Company will follow its company QA/QC procedures which includes performing 
an internal review of the deliverable submittal. This internal process is integral for delivering 
the highest quality product and minimizing risk.

<!-- page 25 -->
Navajo County Public Works  
Joseph City Pedestrian Improvements Project 
pg. 5 
 
 
F. ENVIRONMENTAL DOCUMENT 
There is no additional environmental studies or documentation as part of this contract 
modification. 
 
G. UTILITY COORDINATION AND CLEARANCE 
Wilson & Company will incorporate survey data and translate information included in 
recorded utility drawings to prepare utility base mapping for design. Wilson & Company will 
evaluate existing utility information and coordinate with the Utility Owners and County Staff 
to determine if any potential conflicts or relocations are necessary. Wilson & Company will 
obtain no-conflict utility clearances from all Utility Owners prior to 100% plans. This scope 
of work does not include utility locating (potholing) or utility relocation design since none is 
anticipated for the improvements. 
 
H. RIGHT-OF-WAY SERVICES 
Wilson & Company anticipates all improvements will not require new right-of-way or 
easements. Wilson & Company will obtain the right-of-way clearance letter. 
 
I. POST DESIGN SERVICES 
See original Contact. No additional post design services are part of this contract 
modification. 
 
J. SCHEDULE 
We will begin the additional work upon execution of the contract modification #1 and 
issuance of ‘Notice to Proceed’ to begin the additional services for the design phase of the 
added segments to the original project. The updated project schedule for the Final design 
and development of bid ready construction documentations will be restricted by the 
environmental clearance schedule.  The environmental requirements will be determined at a 
later date and once determined the new schedule will be developed under a separate contract 
modification. 
 
K. SERVICE ELEMENTS NOT INCLUDED 
The following services are not included in this contract modification proposal. If such services are 
required, a contract modification can be executed to Wilson & Company to provide these services: 
▪ Environmental Studies and Documentation 
▪ Geotechnical Engineering services or materials testing 
▪ Right-of-Way Legal Descriptions/Exhibit (no new right-of-way) 
▪ Landscape Architecture/Irrigation 
▪ Off-Site Drainage analysis or design 
▪ Erosion Control 
▪ Traffic Count/Traffic Signal/Traffic Analysis  
▪ Traffic Control/Sequencing  
▪ Public Information Meetings 
▪ Permit Fees 
▪ City/County Review Fees 
▪ Streetlighting Design

<!-- page 26 -->
Navajo County Public Works  
Joseph City Pedestrian Improvements Project 
pg. 6 
 
L. PROFESSIONAL SERVICE FEE 
Wilson & Company will provide the above services for the lump sum fee of $34,843.16 that 
includes project related expenses and delivery costs. The project will bill monthly by percent 
complete.  
Thank you for requesting and selecting Wilson & Company to provide these services. We appreciate 
the business! 
Sincerely, 
WILSON & COMPANY

Title: Pinetop- Lakeside Precinct #6 Constable Appointment 
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Presented By: Melissa Buckley/Rebecca Smith
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and acceptance of the declination of the Precinct #6 Constable appointment of
Dan Price, previously done by the Board of Supervisors, and possible appointment of Robert
Railey to fill the term of the vacant office effective January 23, 2024. 
Background:
Constable Greg Sharp retired December 31, 2023, and this Board, on December 12, 2023,
appointed Dan Price to serve as constable beginning January 1, 2024; Mr. Price, before taking
the oath of office, had some medical conditions that wouldn’t allow him to accept the position.
Former Constable Sharp is now requesting the BOS appoint Robert Railey as constable,
effective today, upon taking the oath of office.
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office and the Show Low Precinct Constable helped with the
Service of Process during this time. 
Mr. Railey is a qualified candidate and requests appointment to the position. 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends acceptance of the declination of Dan Price and appointment of Robert Railey. 
Attachments
Price declination ltr 
Robert Railey Request Ltr 
Form Review
Form Started By: Melissa Buckley Started On: 01/12/2024 02:15 PM
Final Approval Date: 01/12/2024

<!-- page 35 -->
December 27, 2023 
 
 
 
 
Navajo County Board of Directors: 
 
 
  I want to start this letter off by thanking everyone, The Navajo County Board of Directors, my 
friends, and colleges at the Pinetop/Lakeside Justice center for appointing me as their 
Constable for precinct 6. What a great honor it is to know that the work I have done in my 
community has developed this kind of trust and respect. 
 
 It is with a very heavy heart that I must decline my appointment. I recently have been 
experiencing some health issues that is going prevent me from performing my duties. Doctors 
have told me that with some treatment and medications that I should be able to avoid surgery, 
however, have put me on a no stress environment order until my condition is under control. This 
prognosis could take up to six months to a year. 
 
 There is a really good candidate for the position I feel can take my place in the meantime. I ask 
that the board consider Robert Railey as my alternate. Perhaps a year from now things will 
change and I may resume the position? I will most definitely continue to be of service to the 
board and my community in whatever capacity I can until then. 
 
 Again, I cannot express enough gratitude I have towards the Board of Supervisors and the 
people in my community for this opportunity.  
 
 
 
 
Most sincerely, 
 
  
 Dan Price.

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. c.        
Meeting Date: 01/23/2024  
Title: CARE Committee RAOK Proclamation 2024
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Presented By: Ron Smith
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of a Proclamation declaring February 11th - February 17th, 2024 as "Random Acts of
Kindness Week," encouraging our communities and our Navajo County employees to do something kind
for one another.
Background:
The Navajo County CARE Committee's mission is to assist Navajo County employees. The
CARE Committee assists employees during family tragedies, illness etc. The CARE Committee participates
each month by picking 6 names from the birthday list and delivering a mug with assorted candies to
sweeten the employees' month. 
The sole purpose of this week
is to focus on the positivity that surrounds us every day. It's a week to learn, teach, and share our
kindness with others.
Recommendation: 
Recommend adoption of the proclamation. 
Attachments
ROAK Proclamation 
Form Review
Form Started By: Melissa Buckley Started On: 01/17/2024 03:11 PM
Final Approval Date: 01/17/2024

<!-- page 38 -->
Navajo County 
Proclamation 
 
“February 11th – February 17th, 2024 as Random Act of Kindness Week” 
WHEREAS, February is National Random Acts of Kindness Month, making this the perfect 
opportunity to reinforce compassion and empathy in our organization; and  
WHEREAS, A Random Act of Kindness  is deliberately attempting to brighten someone else’s 
day by doing something kind for no specific reason; and  
WHEREAS, every day at Navajo County Complexes, Road Yards, Sub Stations, etc., Random 
Acts of Kindness are performed by the employees of Navajo County; and 
WHEREAS, each act of kindness makes for a higher morale among Navajo County employees 
and inspires more acts of kindness towards others; and 
WHEREAS, many of these Random Acts of Kindness are done anonymously thereby creating 
feelings of goodwill all around us; and 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County CARE Committee participates each month by picking 6 names 
from the birthday list and delivering a mug with assorted candies to sweeten the employees month; 
and 
WHEREAS, in many locations across the County these Random Acts of Kindness have been 
appreciated with positive feedback being shared among others. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT PROCLAIMED, by the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County, 
that the week of February 11th – February 17th, 2024, is designated as Random Acts of Kindness 
Week and we encourage the employees of Navajo County, to join the Board of Supervisors and 
the Navajo County CARE Committee in sharing kindness throughout our work locations and 
beyond. 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on January 23, 2024. 
    
            
Jason E. Whiting, Chairman 
 
ATTEST: 
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 4. a.        
Meeting Date: 01/23/2024  
Title: Broadband Equity, Access, and Deployment Program (BEAD) Challenge No.
B23-09-029
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Administrative Services
Presented By: Grant Evans
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of Contract B23-09-029 for the Broadband Equity, Access, and
Deployment Program (BEAD) Challenge to High Street Consulting Group, LLC and to
authorize change order authority to the County Manager for 10% of the contract amount, and
authorize the County Manager to execute the necessary documents.
Background:
The State of Arizona has received 993.1 million dollars for the BEAD program through the
Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act (IIJA).  The Arizona Commerce Authority (ACA) is
administering this program for the State of Arizona.  The BEAD program and the ACA's top
objective for these funds is to develop high speed broadband infrastructure to serve all
properties that are currently unserved or underserved as defined by the Federal Communications
Commission (FCC).  As part of the BEAD program, the ACA must administer a challenge
process to allow internet service providers, local governments and non-profits an opportunity to
review and challenge, where necessary, the list of addresses that the ACA has currently
determined to be eligible for BEAD funding (those that are either unserved or underserved).  
This contract will provide for a consultant to: 1) assess the existing data from the ACA, 2)
develop and launch a public awareness campaign so that everyone in our County may be
educated, informed and active participants, 3) gather the data from stakeholders and consumers
that is needed to prepare challenges, 4) validate the data gathered, and 5) submit challenges to
the ACA on behalf of Navajo County and our other Governmental partners. 
Through the challenge process, the goal is to assure that all Navajo County properties
(excluding Reservation Properties) are properly identified and that not one single property that
should be eligible for BEAD funding is missed.  
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County
Governmental Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 p.m., M.S.T, November 9, 2023.  No
proposals were accepted after 3:00 p.m.  The proposals were publicly opened and read aloud at
3:15 p.m.  Seven proposals were received and evaluated per the criteria set forth in the request
for qualifications.  Out of the seven proposals, two firms were short-listed and interviewed on
December 4, 2023.

<!-- page 40 -->
Recommendation: 
The Evaluation Committee evaluated the two firms interviewed and is recommending the award
of Contract B23-09-029 Broadband Equity, Access, and Deployment Program (BEAD)
Challenge to High Street Consulting Group, LLC.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 24
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $172,814
Fiscal Impact:
The total amount requested is $172,814.  $157,104 for the consultant's fee and reimbursable
expenses and $15,710 as a reserve fund for project changes.
There are several funding sources and partners that are either committed or anticipated for this
project, and they are described in more detail below.  Financial contributions from these
partners and from the other outside funding sources are not expected until after the project is
underway. The County will pay the consultant fees up front and then receive reimbursement
later.  County funds required for this contract should not exceed $67,814 and these funds are
budgeted from the Local Assistance Consistency Fund. 
There are several partners that have joined the County in collaborating on this project;
including the City of Winslow, the City of Holbrook, the Town of Snowflake, the Town of
Taylor, the City of Show Low, the Town of Pinetop-Lakeside and Northland Pioneer College. 
Each of these partners has verbally agreed to contribute financially to support the project. 
County Staff was also able to receive support for this project from APS and SRP, with each of
them verbally committing to a $25,000 direct contribution.  Additionally, the County submitted
an application for the Utilities Grant Funding Program, an Arizona Public Service, Salt River
Project, and Tucson Electric Power grant funding program for communities impacted by the
current and planned closure of coal-fired generating stations.  Staff is anticipating the award of
a $25,000 grant from this program. The total anticipated partner and grant funding for this
project currently stands at $105,000.

 CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES  
 
AGREEMENT made as of January 23, 2024, between Navajo County ("County") 
and High Street Consulting Group, LLC (“Consultant”). 
 
WHEREAS, County requires certain professiona l services for Broadband Equity 
Access and Deployment (BEAD) Challenge, Contract No. B23-09-029 (“Project”); and 
 
WHEREAS, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.  Consultant shall perform the work  described in the Proposal dated 
December 21, 2023 and those a dditional items set forth in the Consultant’s Fee 
Proposal (Project Budget) dated December 16, 2023, in a competent and professional 
manner to the satisfaction of County.  The Consultant’s Proposal and Fee Proposal is 
attached hereto and by this reference incorporated herein. 
 
2. Contract Documents.
 The Contract Documents comprise this Contract, Request for 
Qualifications No. B23-09-029, Fee Proposal (Project Budget) dated December 16, 
2023, Consultant’s Proposal Submittal dated December 21, 2023, and addendums 
thereto, if any. If any incorporated term is inconsistent with the Contract, this 
Contract shall control over all others; the Request for Qualifications shall control over 
the Consultants proposal. 
 
3. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, 
capacity and professional expertise to perfo rm this Agreement.  Consultant shall 
provide an adequate staff of experienced  personnel, capable of and devoted to the 
successful accomplishment of the work to be performed hereunder.  Consultant shall 
assign specific individuals to key positio ns.  Once assigned to work under this 
Agreement, key personnel shall not be removed or replaced without County’s prior 
written approval.  
 
4. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that  it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall purport to be an agent, employee, partner, joint 
venturer or associate of the other.  An employee or agen t of one party shall not be 
deemed an employer or agent  of the other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social 
Security contributions will not be w ithheld from any paym ent by County, and 
Consultant shall be solely responsible for such matters.

<!-- page 44 -->
Page -2- 
 
5. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without County’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed 
on Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 9, 
10, 11, 13, 16, 19, 22 and 23.  Consultant is responsible for full performance of this 
Agreement regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
6. Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of care and skill ordi narily exercised by me mbers of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care 
and the orderly progress of the Project, and in acco rdance with an agreed upon 
schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a 
schedule approved by County shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by 
County or Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if  necessary, as the Project 
proceeds.  The date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days 
that performance is delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other 
causes beyond Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the 
parties’ representatives. 
 
7. Payment.  County shall pay to Consultant a not-to-exceed sum of one hundred thirty-
seven thousand one hundred and four dolla rs ($137,104.00) for all professional 
services, plus an additional not-to-exceed sum of twenty thousand dollars 
($20,000.00) for all reimbursable expenses as payment in full for all services rendered 
by Consultant pursuant to this Agreement.  Payments shall be made within 30 days of 
County’s receipt of Consultant’s monthly invoices.  Each invoice shall detail the work 
performed during the billing period.  Invoice amounts in percent of Project cost shall 
not exceed the percentage of completion of the Project as approved by County.   
 
8. Defects in Work.  County may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifi cations.  Consultant agrees to promptly 
remedy all such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
controversy or claim through informal nego tiation as set forth in Section 13.  No 
compensation shall be paid for any reject ed work until such issues have been 
resolved. 
 
9. 
Insurance Requirements.   
 
A. Consultant shall maintain in effect , at all times during the term of this 
Agreement, insurance adequate to protect County and its agents, 
representatives, officers, officials and employees against such losses as set 
forth below.  Consultant shall provide County with a current Certificate of 
Insurance or a certified copy of the insurance policy naming County as an 
additional insured (except for Errors an d Omissions / Professional Liability 
coverage).

indemnify and hold harmless County and its  representatives, officers, officials and 
employees (the “Indemnitees”) from and against any and all claims, damages, losses 
and expenses (including but not limited to attorney fees, court costs, and the costs of

including loss of use resulting therefrom, caused in whole or in part by any negligent

by any person for whose negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions Consultant may 
be liable) to the extent of Consultant’s negligence or fault.  The amount and type of 
insurance coverage requirements set fort h above shall not lim it the scope of the

period of five years after completion of the work.  All such documents shall be 
subject to inspection and audit by Coun ty upon reasonable notice during normal 
business hours.  A legible co py of any or all such documents shall be produced by 
Consultant at the request and expense of County.

12. Non-Assignment.  Consultant shall not assign any right or interest in this Agreement

13. Negotiation of Disputes.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any

controversy shall be provided in writi ng, with supporting doc umentation, to the 
recipient designated in Section 25.  The recipient shall have seven calendar days to 
prepare and deliver a written response.  If the parties fail to resolve the disputes 
within a reasonable period of not less than ten days, either party may pursue available 
legal remedies. 
 
14. Suspension and Termination by County: 
 
A. Suspension.  County may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to suspend, 
delay or interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment shall be made 
to the completion date and for any increase in the cost of performance resulting 
from the suspension, delay or interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the 
extent that performance is or would have been suspended, delayed or interrupted 
by another cause for which Consultant is responsible. 
 
B. Termination for Cause.   County may terminate th is Agreement for cause if 
Consultant refuses or fails to supply enough properly skilled workers to perform 
this Agreement; fails to make required payments to subcontractors; disregards 
laws, ordinances, rules, regulations or  orders of a public  authority having 
jurisdiction; or otherwise commits a material breach of  this Agreement.  When 
any of the foregoing causes exist, County shall give Consultant written notice and 
a ten-day opportunity to cure the default.  If the default remains uncured, County 
may (without prejudice to any other rights or remedies it may have) terminate this 
Agreement and finish the work or cause it to be finished.  If the unpaid balance of 
the Agreement exceeds the cost of fini shing the work (including any expenses 
incurred by County as a result of Cons ultant’s default), C onsultant shall be 
entitled to payment for its performance up to the amount of such excess.  If the 
cost of finishing the work exceeds the u npaid balance, Consultant shall pay the 
difference to County. 
 
C. Termination for Convenience.   County, by written n otice to Consultant, may 
terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of County it 
is in County’s best interests to do so.  Consultant shall be pa id for all material, 
equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses and 
a reasonable allowance for profit and overhead, provided that such payments, 
exclusive of termination expenses, shal l not exceed the total amount payable 
pursuant to Section 6 less any paymen ts previously made to Consultant.  
Consultant shall not be entitled to profit and overhead on material, equipment and 
services which were not provided.

1 
Broadband Equity Access and Deployment (BEAD) Challenge  
Contents 
Project Scope................................ ................................ ................................ ..........................  1 
Task 1: Project Management ................................ ................................ ................................ ............ 2 
Task 2: Stakeholder Entity Challenges ................................ ................................ ............................... 2 
Task 3: Survey Portal Development & Direct Mail Campaign ................................ ............................... 4 
Task 4: Processing and Challenge Submission ................................ ................................ .................... 6 
Timeline ................................ ................................ ................................ ................................ . 7 
Project Budget ................................ ................................ ................................ ........................  8 
 
Project Scope 
The consultant team will support Navajo county in completing an effective BEAD challenge. The 
consultant team will support Navajo county in ensuring that all BEAD-eligible locations within the 
county are properly identified by validating existing eligibility information provided by the NTIA, 
FCC, and Arizona Commerce Authority (ACA) and submitting challenges where discrepancies are 
identified. 
Under this scope of work the consultant will prepare challenges based on information provided by 
consumers and information provided by other stakeholders. 
In its Initial Proposal Volume I the ACA has defined a menu of allowable challenge types. These 
are presented in the table below. 
Code Challenge type Description 
A Availability The broadband service identified is not offered at the location 
S Speed The actual speed of the service tier falls below the unserved or 
underserved thresholds 
L Latency The round-trip latency of the broadband service exceeds 100 ms 
D Data cap The only service plans marketed impose an unreasonable data cap 
T Technology The technology indicated for this location is incorrect 
B Business service 
only 
The location is residential, but the service offered is marketed or 
available only to businesses 
E Enforceable 
commitment 
The challenger has knowledge that broadband will be deployed at 
this location by the date established in the deployment obligation. 
P Planned service Broadband will be deployed at this location by June 30, 2024 
N Not part of 
enforceable 
commitment 
The location is not covered by an enforceable commitment 
C Location is a CAI The location should be classified as a CAI 
R Location is not a 
CAI 
The location is currently labeled as a CAI but should not be 
The consultant team will engage members of the public to provide information pertaining to 
availability, speed, latency, data cap, technology, and business service only challenges.

<!-- page 52 -->
2 
Broadband Equity Access and Deployment (BEAD) Challenge  
The consultant team will work with Navajo County and other stakeholder entities to gather 
information support enforceable commitment and community anchor institution (CAI) challenges. 
Task 1: Project Management 
Kickoff Meeting 
Within five days of contract execution a project kickoff meeting will be scheduled to make 
introductions and review the project approach and timeline. 
Project Progress Meetings 
Throughout the project weekly project progress meetings will be held. The meetings will be 
attended, at a minimum, by the consultant project manager and deputy project manager.  
On-Site Meetings 
The consultant public engagement lead and project manager will conduct an in-person meeting 
with the broadband task force. The purpose of the meeting with the broadband task force will be 
to review the field campaign approach and messaging and identify opportunities to use regional 
partnerships and collaboration to promote participation in the consumer broadband speed tests. 
The consultants will meet with or present to other stakeholders during the two-day trip as time 
and schedules allow, such as the county Board of Supervisors and the White Mountain Business 
Owners roundtable. 
The consultant team will prepare materials and an agenda in advance and provide meeting 
minutes. 
Communication, Coordination & Project Management 
Regular updates and collaboration with stakeholders will be maintained throughout the project to 
ensure transparency and address any emerging issues. Navajo County’s cooperation and feedback 
is highly valued to ensure the success of this initiative. The project manager will ensure that tasks 
are assigned and completed according to the project schedule and meeting all quality standards. 
The consultant will submit invoices at specified milestones.  
Task 2: Stakeholder Entity Challenges 
Map Development 
The consultant team will prepare a securely hosted map with all data layers pertinent to 
operating a successful challenge process: 
• Broadband Serviceable Locations, classified as BEAD eligible (unserved and underserved 
not covered by an existing enforceable commitment) and BEAD ineligible (served or 
covered by an enforceable commitment) 
• Existing enforceable commitments (federal and state broadband funding programs) 
• Existing advertised broadband services (based on the FCC Broadband Data Collection 
data) 
• Ookla Speed Test Results 
• Community Anchor Institutions 
The consultant team will set up and provide secure access to the map to Navajo County staff and 
designated stakeholders. At the conclusion of the project the map and data layers will be 
transitioned to Navajo County for their long term use. 
 
The consultant team will perform a series of targeted interviews or inquiries as directed by Navajo

<!-- page 53 -->
3 
Broadband Equity Access and Deployment (BEAD) Challenge  
County to gather information from stakeholders and subject matter experts about where the 
information displayed may be inaccurate or incomplete. 
Enforceable Commitment Evaluations 
The BEAD Program prohibits treating locations covered by another federal, state, or local grant 
program to deliver qualifying broadband service as “unserved” or “underserved.” However, the 
NTIA Assistant Secretary may: 
waive such treatment of locations or areas with prior enforceable commitments 
at the request of the Eligible Entity in cases where the Eligible Entity can 
demonstrate to the satisfaction of the Assistant Secretary that such treatment 
of such locations or areas is necessary to achieve the goals of the program, 
including where purported commitments do not have the appropriate 
documentation with respect to Tribal lands consistent with [program] 
requirements. 
The Arizona Commerce Authority may also provide funding to locations covered under an existing 
enforceable commitment when locations “will not be served by qualifying broadband service as a 
result of [existing] enforceable commitment.” 
 
As directed by Navajo County the consultant team will investigate the status of existing 
enforceable commitments. This may include evaluation of the plausibility of existing forecasts and 
plans, willingness of project partners to participate, or the plausibility of financial forecasts. All 
meetings and interactions will be documented. Where these interactions reveal a lack of 
credibility to fulfill commitments under existing federal and state grant programs (which render 
within Navajo County as being ineligible for BEAD program funding), the consultant team will 
prepare an Enforceable Commitment challenge for Navajo County review and potential 
submission to the ACA. 
 
Community Anchor Institution Evaluation 
The consultant team will review existing designated Community Anchor Institutions for 
completeness and accuracy.

<!-- page 54 -->
4 
Broadband Equity Access and Deployment (BEAD) Challenge  
Task 3: Survey Portal Development & Direct Mail Campaign 
Consumer-information-based challenges will be generated by a field campaign to engage 
broadband subscribers (or potential subscribers) to provide information that can be used to 
submit challenge information to the Arizona Commerce Authority (ACA). This consumer-provided 
information will be compiled, validated, and—upon approval by Navajo County—submitted to the 
ACA. Our approach can be broken down into three steps, executed in rapid succession: 
1. Preparation 
2. Field Campaign 
3. Challenge Submission 
Our public outreach will gather evidence from the public to create successful challenges, focused 
on availability, speed, latency, and data caps. The evidence to support these challenges will be 
either a completed survey form with a speed test result or attached documentation, such as a 
screen shot of a provider website or a PDF of a letter. 
Survey Mailing List Development 
The consultant team will begin by developing a list 
of all locations for which to assess broadband 
access. The FCC defines any location eligible for a 
mass-market broadband service as a “broadband 
serviceable location” (BSL). This includes both 
residential and business locations. The Arizona 
Commerce Authority will publish a list of BEAD 
eligible locations in January 2024. This will include 
all potentially BEAD eligible locations (unserved, 
underserved, and served).  
Unserved eligible locations require no further 
action. Current FCC data indicates about 30% of 
locations in Navajo County are unserved. 
Underserved locations constitute an additional 
~10% of locations in Navajo County. Underserved 
locations will receive second priority for BEAD funding after unserved locations. At Navajo 
County’s discretion, underserved locations can be included in the target location group to allow 
for the possibility that some underserved locations are actually unserved.  
Areas covered under existing enforceable commitment (that is, another federal or state 
broadband grant program such as RDOF or CAF II) will be excluded from our target outreach 
areas, since these areas are not eligible for BEAD program funding. 
High Street’s data scientists will join this list of locations to the FCC Location Fabric data 
(produced by CostQuest Associates and distributed by the FCC to eligible entities, including 
Navajo County), which will enrich this locations data with addresses, designation as urban or 
rural, business or residential, and if residential, the number of dwelling units contained within. 
Additionally, the FCC Fixed Broadband Availability data will be joined to the dataset to 
summarize the FCC’s records of existing broadband service. 
The result of this step will be a target list of broadband serviceable locations (BSLs) which could 
be included in a challenge based on the results of this process. We estimate this number to be 
What about locations missing from the ACA 
list? 
The ACA list of BEAD eligible locations is based 
on the FCC Fabric dataset. The ACA challenge 
process, as described in the ACA Initial Proposal 
Volume I, does not include a challenge category 
for missing locations. Since the primary scope of 
this engagement is to prepare an ACA challenge, 
we will focus on the accuracy of the served / 
unserved / underserved classification of existing 
locations. To the extent that our marketing 
campaign identifies missing locations, 
individuals at those locations will be directed to 
the FCC’s website to submit a location 
challenge.

<!-- page 55 -->
5 
Broadband Equity Access and Deployment (BEAD) Challenge  
between in the vicinity of 15,000 – 20,000 locations (excluding tribal areas). These locations will 
constitute our target market.  
Survey Portal Development & Email Automation 
High Street will develop an online survey to guide participants in submitting evidence to support a 
challenge. We will solicit screenshot, letters, or emails that identified broadband service is not 
offered at the location or is associated with unreasonable data caps, and speed test results (using 
the integrated speed test) that would support a speed or latency challenge. The survey website 
will provide simple and clear instructions to users describing the types of applicable challenges 
and the corresponding documentation that can be provided to substantiate a challenge.  
The survey website will be connected to the target locations database so that a user can enter 
their location ID (if receiving a postcard) or their address and have the survey automatically 
populated with the challengeable data. This will make it faster and more convenient for survey 
takers and improve the accuracy of the challenge data and make it easy for subscribers to 
indicate the speed tier of their subscribed service. 
The speed and latency test will conform to the requirements established by the Arizona Commerce 
Authority. The test will be provided using a licensed Ookla SpeedTest Custom speed test 
integrated into an Esri Survey123 survey to capture respondent information. Embedded into the 
survey, the survey will automatically capture required data elements including the user’s IP 
address, time and date, name and street address, certification of the speed tier the customer 
subscribes to, and agreement to use the supplied data to support the challenge process. 
Collateral Development & Design 
High Street’s communications and public outreach team will develop overall campaign messaging 
and specific collateral text to communicate with the public the importance and value of their 
participation in the survey and data collection. In in-house team of graphic designers will support 
the collateral development to ensure that the visuals are as engaging as the written content. 
 
Print & Mail Postcards 
High Street’s field campaign will reach individuals primarily through direct mail. The FCC Fabric 
dataset provides us with the physical address of every location eligible to be challenged with the 
ACA. 
 
The primary outreach method will be mailed postcards to each BSL with a customized description 
of the FCC’s records of broadband service availability and service status. The postcards and 
survey will explain the necessity of accurate coverage information to ensure eligibility for future 
broadband service expansion. The postcard will include three key elements: 1) a strong call to 
action (“complete the survey to help connect Navajo County”); 2) classification and service 
availability information; and 3) a link and QR code to the survey website. 
We will work with our direct mail partner to use variable data printing to customize each postcard 
to the individual recipient with: a) challengeable FCC data about available internet services at 
their location; b) whether they are considered served or not (that is, whether or not the BEAD 
program will help bring broadband internet to their location); c) the 8-digit Location ID that, when 
(optionally) entered into the survey will pre-populate the survey with the complete FCC data; d) 
the website address and a QR code to the survey; and, e) a phone number connected to a Google 
Voice or similar phone service where individuals can text an SMS chatbot or call and leave a 
voicemail if they do not have internet access.

<!-- page 56 -->
6 
Broadband Equity Access and Deployment (BEAD) Challenge  
We anticipate direct mail costs of approximately $0.47 per postcard sent, including printing and 
postage.  
 
Press Release & Other Printed Materials 
The consultant team will prepare a press release for Navajo County to distribute to its media 
partners as part of the outreach reach effort. Talking points will be developed for the project 
spokesperson(s) for interviews. 
The consultant team will assist Navajo County with additional outreach to produce additional 
survey responses, such as local PSAs, utility bill inserts, or earned media opportunities. 
Task 4: Processing and Challenge Submission 
We anticipate launching our field campaign on or around February 1st to and be receiving 
responses and evidence for challenges shortly thereafter. It will be our job as the consultant team 
to receive the evidence submitted by the public and translate this evidence into valid challenges. 
High Street’s survey tool that is associated to the FCC-identified Location IDs will reduce work on 
the backend to review and prepare challenges. 
Challenge Portal Access and Submission Methods 
The consultant team will establish access to the ACA’s challenge portal and develop the technical 
methods for batch, bulk, or programmatic submission of challenges to the portal. 
We anticipate being able to programmatically submit accepted challenges to the ACA’s challenge 
portal using an Application Programming Interface (API) or other programmatic fashion to reduce 
costs and the possibility for error. We have cost-effective resources identified internally for 
conducting the reviews to ensure that they’re processed quickly and accurately. 
While the survey is in the field we will develop a simple back-end web application to make it fast 
and effective to review the challenges as they come in. Speed and latency challenges will be 
automatically joined to the Census Block containing the location.  
The IP address, name, and address of survey respondents are considered personally identifiable 
information (PII). This information will be stored securely and not released to the public. 
Review and Screen Survey Results 
No internet? Call or text 
“internet” to (928) 555-5555

<!-- page 57 -->
7 
Broadband Equity Access and Deployment (BEAD) Challenge  
Survey results will be reviewed and screened to ensure that speed test results are valid evidence 
of a challenge. For example, if a fixed wireless subscriber submits results showing underserved 
speeds but is within an area served by a fiber provider that offers gigabit symmetric speeds, this 
would not support reclassification form served to underserved. Availability challenges will be 
manually reviewed and accepted, rejected, or flagged for further consideration at the end of the 
survey period.  
Challenge Review and Submission 
In collaboration with Navajo County, the consultant team will establish a schedule for when 
challenges are submitted. Ideally, we would propose to submit challenges on a rolling basis. 
Submitting challenges before the end of the period will enable the team to gain feedback on the 
effectiveness of challenges submitted and any rebuttals as well as reducing any risk of not 
finishing on time. Speed and latency challenges will be held until at least three cumulative 
challenges on three different days of the same provider within the same Census block are received 
or the end of the survey period. 
All challenges will be reviewed and approved by Navajo County prior to submission. The 
consultant team will be responsible for challenge submission. 
 
Timeline 
Our proposed project timeline is presented below. Our familiarity with BEAD program data (FCC 
Fabric data, etc.), deploying Survey123 surveys into the field, and the ACA challenge process will 
enable us to hit the ground running with buffer built in to ensure timely submission the challenges. 
Our contracts team is standing at the ready to ensure that, if selected, our team can turn around 
all contract documents and begin working immediately. 
 
We’ve front-loaded our work early in the ACA Challenge Process window to ensure that there is a 
buffer within the ACA’s challenge window, should any unexpected delays be encountered. 
This timeline is subject to change to be responsive to the ACA’s finalized challenge process 
schedule.

<!-- page 58 -->
8 
Broadband Equity Access and Deployment (BEAD) Challenge  
Project Budget 
 
The project will be completed by the consultant on a fixed-price, lump sum basis for all labor 
costs. The consultant will invoice on a “percent complete” basis at the following milestones: 
• Task 2 Completion: 30% of labor budget 
• Task 3 Completion: 40% of labor budget 
• Task 4 Completion: 30% of labor budget 
Other Direct Costs (ODCs) will be directly reimbursable. The consultant will provide receipts for all 
ODCs and will be reimbursed at cost (no markup). Total reimbursable ODCs are estimated at 
$17,028 based on an estimated 20,000 printed and mailed postcards. The actual number of 
postcards may be lesser or greater than this amount. Total reimbursable ODCs shall not exceed 
$20,000.

Broadband Equity Access and Deployment (BEAD) Challenge

Board of Supervisors Regular 5. a. 1.        
Meeting Date: 01/23/2024  
Title: IGA CTR068851 Sexually Transmitted Infection Investigation
Submitted For: Shirlene Newby, Interim Deputy Director of Clinical Services 
Submitted By: Shirlene Newby, Interim Deputy Director of Clinical Services
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
Renewal of Intergovernmental Agreement, Contract No. CTR068851, between Arizona
Department of Health Services and NCPHSD for the investigation and treatment of sexually
transmitted infections for the renewal period from January 1, 2024, to December 31, 2024
Background:
This is an ongoing IGA with ADHS for strengthening sexually transmitted Disease Prevention
and Control in Arizona. This renewal period is for 1/1/2024-12/31/2024.  Funding utilized
towards prevention, education, testing and treatment of STIs, as well as toward contact tracing
and partner referral services.

1. Definition of Terms As used in this Contract, the terms listed below are defined as follows: 
 
As used in this Contract, the terms listed below are defined as follows: 
 
1.1 “Attachment” means any item in the Contract which requires the Contractor to submit as part of the Offer. 
 
1.2 “Contract” means the combination of the Contract documents, including the Terms and Conditions, and 
the Specifications and Statement or Scope of Work; and any Contract Amendments. 
 
1.3 "Contract Amendment" means a written document signed by the Procurement Officer that is issued for the 
purpose of making changes in the Contract. 
 
1.4 “Contractor” means any person who has a Contract with the State. 
 
1.5 “Data” means recorded information, regardless of form or the media on which it may be recorded. The 
term may include technical data and computer software. The term does not include information incidental 
to contract administration, such as financial, administrative, cost or pricing, or management information. 
 
1.6 “Days” means calendar days unless otherwise specified. 
 
1.7 “Exhibit” means any item labeled as an Exhibit in the Contract generally containing maps, schematics, 
examples of reports, or other documents that will be used to perform the requirements of the Scope of 
Work after contract award. 
 
1.8 “Gratuity” means a payment, loan, subscription, advance, deposit of money, services, or anything of more 
than nominal value, present or promised, unless consideration of substantially equal or greater value is 
received. 
 
1.9 “Materials” means all property, including equipment, supplies, printing, insurance and leases of property 
but does not include land, a permanent interest in land or real property or leasing space. 
 
1.10 “Procurement Officer” means the person, or his or her designee, duly authorized by the State to enter into 
and administer Contracts and make written determinations with respect to the Contract. 
 
1.11 “Services” means the furnishing of labor, time or effort by a Contractor or Subcontractor which does not 
involve the delivery of a specific end product other than required reports and performance, but does not 
include employment agreements or collective bargaining agreements. 
 
1.12 “State” means any department, commission, council, board, bureau, committee, institution, agency, 
government corporation or other establishment or official of the executive branch or corporation 
commission of the State of Arizona that executes the Contract. 
 
1.13 “State Fiscal Year” means the period beginning with July 1 and ending June 30. 
 
1.14 “Subcontract” means any Contract, express or implied, between the Contractor and another party or 
between a Subcontractor and another party delegating or assigning, in whole or in part, the making or 
furnishing of any Materials or any Services required for the performance of the Contract. 
 
1.15 “Subcontractor” means a person who contracts to perform work or render Services to a Contractor or to 
another Subcontractor as a part of a Contract with the State.

3. Contract Interpretation 
 
3.1. Arizona Law. The Arizona law applies to this Contract including, where applicable, the Uniform 
Commercial Code as adopted by the State of Arizona and the Arizona Procurement Code, Arizona 
Revised Statutes (A.R.S.) Title 41, Chapter 23, and its implementing rules, Arizona Administrative Code 
(A.A.C.) Title 2, Chapter 7; 
 
3.2. Implied Contract Terms. Each provision of law and any terms required by law to be in this Contract are 
a part of this Contract as if fully stated in it; 
 
3.3. Contract Order of Precedence. In the event of a conflict in the provisions of the Contract, as accepted 
by the State and as they may be amended, the following shall prevail in the order set forth below: 
 
3.3.1. Terms and Conditions, 
 
3.3.2. Statement or Scope of Work, 
 
3.3.3. Specifications, 
 
3.3.4. Attachments, 
 
3.3.5. Exhibits, then 
 
3.3.6. Any other documents referenced or included in the Contract including, but not limited to, any 
documents that do not fall into one (1) of the above categories. 
 
3.4. Relationship of Parties. The Contractor under this Contract is an independent Contractor. Neither party 
to this Contract shall be deemed to be the employee or agent of the other party to the Contract; 
 
3.5. Severability. The provisions of this Contract are severable. Any term or condition deemed illegal or 
invalid shall not affect any other term or condition of the Contract; 
 
3.6. No Parole Evidence. This Contract is intended by the parties as a final and complete expression of their 
agreement. No course of prior dealings between the parties and no usage of the trade shall supplement 
or explain any terms used in this document and no other understanding either oral or in writing shall be 
binding; and 
 
3.7. No Waiver. Either party’s failure to insist on strict performance of any term or condition of the Contract 
shall not be deemed a waiver of that term or condition even if the party accepting or acquiescing in the 
nonconforming performance knows of the nature of the performance and fails to object to it. 
 
4. Contract Administration and Operation 
 
4.1. Term.  As indicated on the signature page of the Contract, the Contract shall be effective as of the Begin 
Date and shall remain effective until the Termination Date; 
 
4.2. Contract Renewal.  This Contract shall not bind, nor purport to bind, the State for any contractual 
commitment in excess of the original Contract period.  The term of the Contract shall not exceed five (5) 
years.  However, if the original Contract period is for less than five (5) years, the State shall have the
