Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 
RESOLUTION ____-24 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS APPROVING 
A SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR AZ11-173 HUNTRESS WIRELESS COMMUNICATION 
FACILITY 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit was duly filed on October 24th 
2023 by Graham Chapman to allow for the construction of a one hundred and five foot  (105’) 
monopole wireless communication tower and associated facility on a 12.43 acre parcel, in the 
Winslow area; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN # 103-23-010A, Township 19 North, Range 16 East, Section 06 of the Gila and Salt River 
Base and Meridian; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on December 14th, 2023; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony 
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of Staff, found that 
the Special Use Permit is consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and should 
be approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supervisors  hereby 
approves the Special Use Permit, subject to the following conditions:  
 
1. This Special Use Permit shall permit 1 (one) monopole wireless communication 
tower, not to exceed 105 feet in height, and accessory structures necessary for 
operation of the tower on the subject property. 
 
2. The applicant shall obtain a Building Permit from Navajo County for such tower and 
facilities prior to commencing construction. 
 
3. The applicant shall meet all State and Federal requirements concerning 
communication towers prior to any building permits being issued. 
 
4. The tower shall be constructed as a monopole, as indicated in the supplied plans.  
The owner of the tower shall maintain the tower, antennae as necessary. 
 
5. A fence of material acceptable to the Planning and Development Services Director shall be 
constructed to enclose the base of the tower and necessary accessory structures.  
 
6. A Geotechnical Report for the Access Easement shall be submitted and approved.

<!-- page 361 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
7. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site 
plan.  Any expansion or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to 
this permit. 
 
8. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period, from 
the date of Board approval, this permit shall become null and void. 
 
9. Should the operator decide to abandon the use of the tower, they shall notify Navajo 
County Planning and Development Services that the tower is no longer being 
utilized. 
 
10. If the tower is no longer being utilized, the property owner shall cause, at their 
expense, the removal of all components of this tower within a 90-day period. 
 
11. The owner shall provide reasonable cooperation to other public and private users 
who may be interested in collocation on this tower and shall consider such 
collocation upon commercially reasonable terms, unless such collocation is not 
technically feasible. 
 
12. This Special Use Permit shall be personal to the permittee and applicable only to the 
specific use and to the specific property for which it is issued. Upon completion and final 
inspection by the Planning and Development Services Director (or designee) of any 
authorized structures, signifying that all zoning and site development requirements 
imposed in connection with the permit have been satisfied, the Special Use Permit shall 
thereafter be transferable and shall run with the land, whereupon the maintenance of special 
conditions imposed by the permit, as well as the compliance with other provisions of this 
title, shall be the responsibility of the property owner. 
 
13. This Special Use Permit and the subject site shall be reviewed and inspected by Planning 
and Development Staff on the fifth (5th) anniversary of Board of Supervisors approval and 
every five (5) years thereafter to ensure the site is being utilized as approved and is in 
compliance with all conditions contained herein.  If the subject site is found to be out of 
compliance with these conditions, this Special Use Permit and its privileges may be 
revoked. 
 
PASSED AND ADOPTED  by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of 
_____ yeas and ______ nays on this 23rd day of January, 2024.  
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
  
ATTEST:                                                        By ________________________________________ 
                                                                            Jason Whiting, Chairman of the Board 
________________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

<!-- page 362 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 
RESOLUTION ____-24 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS DENYING A 
SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR AZ11-173 HUNTRESS WIRELESS COMMUNICATION 
FACILITY 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit was duly filed on October 24th 
2023 by Graham Chapman to allow for the construction of a one hundred and five  foot (105’) 
monopole wireless communication tower and associated facility on a 12.43 acre parcel, in the 
Winslow area; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN # 103-23-010A, Township 19 North, Range 16 East, Section 06 of the Gila and Salt River 
Base and Meridian; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on December 14th, 2023; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony 
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of Staff, found that 
the Special Use Permit is not consistent with the public health, safety and general wel fare and 
should be denied; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED the Board of Supervisors hereby deny 
the Special Use Permit. 
 
DENIED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of _____ yeas and 
______ nays on this 23rd day of January, 2024.  
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
 By ________________________________________

NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT

STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: January 23, 2024 
Case No.:  TXT-A 23-005 
Action:   Zoning Ordinance Text Amendment  
Project Name: Amendment to Articles 15 and 30 – Swap Meets 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
Request Description: Consideration of a text amendment to Article 15, Section 1502 and Article 30, 
Section 3002 regarding swap meets. 
 
Recommendation: The Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission and Navajo County 
Planning and Development Services Staff recommends approval of the Text Amendment as proposed 
by the track changes and clean copies attached. 
 
BACKGROUND 
 
The Navajo County Zoning Ordinance provides a guideline for land development within the 
community. As evident by recent inquiries from the public and Code Enforcement cases, the Navajo 
County Planning and Development Staff have determined that a need exists for the addition of 
definitions and usages for swap meets.  
 
ANALYSIS 
 
Staff has researched the zoning ordinances from several different jurisdictions to develop the definitions 
and usages as proposed.  Within this research, as it pertains to swap meets, Staff identified that it was 
commonplace to include auctions alongside swap meets and chose to include auctions within the usages 
as amended, as both uses employ similar procedures and facilities. 
 
Planning and Development Services Staff have  received no public comments for the text amendment 
as of January 11th, 2024.

Title: TXT-A 23-006 Amendment to Article 25 - Accessory Building Setbacks, and
Accessory Dwelling Units
Submitted For: Sondra Starke, Planner I  Submitted By: Sondra Starke, Planner I
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Development
Presented By: Sondra Starke
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 05-2024,
approving/denying text amendment to Article 25, Section 2503, regarding accessory building
setbacks, and Article 25, Section 2520, regarding accessory dwelling units.
Background:
The Navajo County Zoning Ordinance provides a guideline for land development within the
community. As evident by recent inquiries from the public and Code Enforcement cases, the
Navajo County Planning and Development Staff have determined that a need exists for the
Zoning Ordinance to update the three-foot (3’) accessory building setback to a five-foot (5’)
setback from property lines and other buildings on each side of the accessory building in order
to require compliance with fire code. There was also a reference to servant or caretaker quarters
remaining following the previous removal of these uses that have been replaced with guest
houses, which Staff recommends striking. Additionally, the Arizona Revised Statutes were
updated to include park models as permittable accessory dwellings, and the Zoning Ordinance
must be updated into compliance. Staff has proposed to include all zoning districts which allow
for accessory dwellings as the new State law has provided a mechanism to permit park models
as accessory dwelling units in a similar manner to manufactured homes.
Recommendation: 
The Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission and Navajo County Planning and
Development Staff recommend approval of the Text Amendment, as proposed by the track
changes and clean copies attached.

NAVAJO N  
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: January 23, 2024 
Case No.:  TXT-A 23-006 
Action:   Zoning Ordinance Text Amendment  
Project Name: Amendment to Article 25 – Accessory Building Setbacks, and Accessory 
Dwelling Units 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
Request Description: Consideration of a text amendment to Article 25, Section 2503, regarding 
accessory building setbacks, and Article 25, Section 2520, regarding accessory dwelling units. 
 
Recommendation: The Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission and Navajo County 
Planning and Development Staff recommend approval of the Text Amendment, as proposed by the 
track changes and clean copies attached. 
 
BACKGROUND/ANALYSIS 
 
The Navajo County Zoning Ordinance provides a guideline for land development within the 
community. As evident by recent inquiries from the public and Code Enforcement cases, the Navajo 
County Planning and Development Staff have determined that a need exists for the Zoning Ordinance 
to update the three-foot (3’) accessory building setback to a five -foot (5’) setback from property lines 
and other buildings on each side of the accessory building in order to require compliance with fire code. 
There was also a reference to servant or caretaker quarters remaining following the previous removal 
of these uses that have been replaced with guest houses, which Staff recommends striking. Additionally, 
the Arizona Revised Statutes were updated to include park models as permittable accessory dwellings, 
and the Zoning Ordinance must be updated into compliance. Staff has proposed to include all zoning 
districts which allow for accessory dwellings as the new State law has provided a mechanism to permit 
park models as accessory dwelling units in a similar manner to manufactured homes.  
 
Planning and Development Services Staff have  received no public comment s for the project as of 
January 11th, 2024.

PLANNING AND DEVELOPMENT SERVICES RECOMMENDATION  
 
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff recommend approval of the proposed amendments 
to Article 25 within the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance.

2. Clean version with all changes accepted for Article 25

<!-- page 393 -->
Board of Supervisors              3 | P a g e  
 
Attachment 1 – Track Changes 
 
 
Article 25 is hereby amended as follows (new text is shown in underline, and text proposed to be deleted is 
shown as strikeout): 
 
Section 2503 – Accessory Buildings and Uses. 
1. Accessory buildings shall not be constructed upon a lot until the construction of the principal 
building has been actually commenced, and accessory buildings shall not be used for dwelling 
purposes for other than servants and caretakers employed on the premises.  
2. Accessory buildings may be built in the required rear or side yard but such accessory buildings shall 
not occupy more than thirty percent (30%)of the required rear yard and shall not be nearer than two 
(2) five (5) feet to any side or rear lot line or setback line, except that in the case of corner lots, 
accessory buildings shall not be nearer to the street than a distance equal to not less than one half 
(1/2) of the depth of the required front yard of the corner lot and when a garage is entered from an 
alley, it shall not be located nearer than ten (10) feet to the alley line. 
 
 
Section 2520 – Guest Houses 
 
ADD in the appropriate alphabetical and numerical sequence: Park models may be used as a Guest House in 
zones where guest houses are permissible, provided they are placed on a permanent or semi-permanent 
foundation. All other permitting requirements shall apply to park models.

<!-- page 394 -->

purposes. 
2. Accessory buildings may be built in the required rear or side yard but shall not be nearer than five (5)

buildings shall not be nearer to the street than a distance equal to not less than one half (1/2) of the 
depth of the required front yard of the corner lot and when a garage is entered from an alley, it shall

Section 2520 – Guest Houses

foundation. All other permitting requirements shall apply to park models.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. a. 4.        
Meeting Date: 01/23/2024  
Title: TXT-A 23-007 Amendment to Article 25 – Prohibition Against
Placement/Movement of Mobile Homes 20 Years Old or Older
Submitted For: Sondra Starke, Planner I  Submitted By: Sondra Starke, Planner I
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Development
Presented By: Sondra Starke
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number
06-2024, approving/denying text amendment to Article 25, Section 2517 of the Navajo County
Zoning Ordinance, regarding age of mobile homes which require rehabilitation verification.
Background:
The Arizona Department of Housing and Urban Development (HUD) requires all mobile homes
of an indicated age to be certified as rehabilitated in accordance with current HUD-established
safety standards, in accordance with R4-34-606. The Navajo County Zoning Ordinance
currently reflects the HUD-indicated age at which mobile homes must obtain certification of
rehabilitation as having been prior to June 15, 1976. Navajo County updated to the 2018
IRC/IBC on June 22, 2022 to reflect modern safety standards. As a result, the proposed Zoning
Ordinance Text Amendment attempts to bridge the gap between 1976 standards and 2018
standards for mobile home safety assurance upon relocation of older dwellings.
Recommendation: 
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff recommends approval of the Text
Amendment as proposed by the track changes and clean copies attached.

NAVAJO N 
 
 
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: January 23, 2024 
Case No.:  TXT-A 23-007 
Action:   Zoning Ordinance Text Amendment  
Project Name: Amendment to Article 25 – Prohibition Against Placement/Movement of 
Mobile Homes 20 Years Old or Older 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
Request Description: Consideration of text amendment to Article 25, Section 2517 of the Navajo 
County Zoning Ordinance, regarding age of mobile home which requires HUD rehabilitation 
certification. 
 
Recommendation: Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff recommends approval 
of the Text Amendment as proposed by the track changes and clean copies attached. 
 
BACKGROUND 
 
The Arizona Department of Housing and Urban Development (HUD) requires all mobile homes of a n 
indicated age to be certified as rehabilitated in accordance with current HUD -established safety 
standards, in accordance with R4-34-606. The Navajo County Zoning Ordinance currently reflects the 
HUD-indicated age at which mobile homes must obtain certification of r ehabilitation as having been 
prior to June 15, 1976. Navajo County updated to the 2018 IRC/IBC on June 22, 2022 to reflect modern 
safety standards. As a result, the proposed Zoning Ordinance Text Amendment attempts to bridge the 
gap between 1976 standards and 2018 standards for mobile home safety assurance upon relocation of 
older dwellings. 
 
ANALYSIS 
 
The efforts put forth by Navajo County to promote safety and wellness within the community by 
adopting the 2018 IRC and 2018 IBC have clarified that all development in the community shall be held 
to a regulated and modern standard of quality. In effect, mo bile home movement must also be done in 
a regulated manner to ensure that Navajo County is a safe county in which to reside. Navajo County has 
discovered a discrepancy within which mobile homes manufactured between June of 1976 and the 
current day that wou ld benefit from HUD certification to verify safe living standards and assist 
homeowners in rehabilitation awareness are being relocated in their uncertified states. This gap in 
coverage provides for unsafe homes to remain unsafe, whereas updating the requi rement to a rolling

<!-- page 405 -->
Board of Supervisors              2 | P a g e  
 
20-year basis would provide for an opportunity to ensure that the mobile homes to be relocated are safe 
and habitable by modern standards. 
  
Staff has determined through research that, by continuing to follow the HUD -indicated age marker of 
June 22, 1976, there is a considerable concern with the above discrepancy, which validates interest in 
adjusting the age threshold by a minimum of 20 years on a rolling basis.  
  
Based upon the above, Staff is currently recommending that the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance be 
updated to require all mobile homes 20 years old or older to be either certified as rehabilitated by HUD 
if 1976 or older or rehabilitation verified by Navajo County if manufactured after 1976  and permitted 
by Navajo County prior to relocation.  
 
Within the attachments of this Staff report, the Board of Supervisors will find a track changes and clean 
copy version of the amendments, as currently proposed by Staff. 
 
Planning and Development Services Staff have  received no public comments for the text amendment 
as of January 11th, 2024.

Attachment 1 – Track Changes 
 
 
Article 25 is hereby amended as follows (new text is shown in underline, and text proposed to be deleted is 
shown as strikeout): 
 
Section 2517 – Prohibition Against Placement/Movement of Mobile Homes 20 Years Old or Older 
 
Notwithstanding any other provision of the Zoning Ordinance, from and after April 1, 2023 March 1, 2024, 
no Mobile Home 20 years old or older at the time of application for mobile home, manufactured home 
permit shall be located within the unincorporated area of Navajo County. This prohibition shall not apply to 
any Mobile Home lawfully located within the unincorporated area of Navajo County as of April 1, 2023- 
March 1, 2024, but it shall apply to any such Mobile Home which is thereafter relocated to another location 
within the unincorporated area of Navajo County. This prohibition shall likewise not apply to any Mobile 
Home that has been certified as rehabilitated to HUD (Department of Housing and Urban Development) 
standards by the State Office of Manufactured Housing or its designee in accordance with Board of 
Manufactured Housing Regulation Number R4-34-107, Rehabilitation of Mobile Homes, which is required 
by Navajo County for all mobile homes manufactured during or prior to 1976. If a Mobile Home lawfully 
located in the unincorporated area of Navajo County as of April 1, 2023 March 1, 2024 is thereafter to be 
relocated to another location within the unincorporated area of Navajo County, the owner shall obtain HUD 
rehabilitation certification and relevant County permits prior to such relocation. rehabilitation verification 
and any relevant permits from the Navajo County Building Division prior to such relocation.

Background/Analysis
• HUD requires all mobile homes manufactured July 15, 1976, or 
older to obtain rehabilitation certification
• To bridge the gap between 1976 and 2018 standards, Navajo 
County intends to verify the same rehabilitation standards set 
by HUD prior to the relocation of any manufactured home 
within Navajo County
